If information conflicts:

Documents override chat memory

Later timestamps override earlier

No assumptions are allowed

If unsure → ask before proceeding.

2. Locked Definitions (Must Not Drift)

These concepts are canon-locked and must not be redefined:

Answer Sets

Single: One specific instance

Equivalent:
Same staff note AND same octave, mapped to all valid fretboard locations producing that exact pitch
(octave differences are NOT equivalent)

Octave (future): Same note name across a defined octave range

All (future): All instances of the same note name

Display vs Visibility

Boards are always visible

Display = persistence of correct answers after marking

Display is configurable per surface

Cardinality / Answer Count

Answer Set ≠ Answer Count

Default:
Answer Set = Single
Answer Count = 1
Single instance

Surface Scope

Surface behavior is configurable per surface and per role:

Prompt

Required Input

Allowed Input

Persistence

Each surface supports:

single

rotating

all

random

3. Presets A–J (Locked Intent)

The following presets are the baseline required presets and are already defined in prior chat. They must be preserved exactly unless explicitly revised:

A–G: Note Rail → Fretboard / Staff / TAB (various combinations)

H–J: Board → Note Rail (recognition direction)

No renaming.
Descriptions may be clarified but not reinterpreted.

Locked definitions (brief restatement)
Answer Sets

Single: one specific instance.

Equivalent: same staff note and same octave, mapped to all valid fretboard locations producing that exact pitch (octave differences are not equivalent).

Octave (future): same note name across a defined octave range.

All (future): all instances of the same note name.

Display vs Visibility

Boards are always visible.

Display = persistence of correct answers after marking (configurable per surface).

Cardinality / Answer Count

Answer Set ≠ Answer Count.

Default: Answer Set = Single, Answer Count = 1.

Surface Scope

Configurable per surface and per role (Prompt, Required Input, Allowed Input, Persistence).

Supported scopes: single, rotating, all, random.

Presets A–J

Names and intent are locked. No renaming or reinterpretation.

Current phase confirmation
Phase B-2 — Preset Definition Table + JSON Schema
Objective: define presets A–J and a schema encoding surfaces, answer rules, scope, and persistence. No engine wiring.

Preset Definition Table (Canon-aligned)
Abbreviations: NR = Note Rail, FB = Fretboard, ST = Staff, TB = TAB
Default unless stated: Answer Set = Single, Answer Count = 1, Display (persistence) = off on prompt surface, on where historically displayed is required by preset intent.

Preset	Prompt Surface(s)	Required Input Surface(s)	Allowed Input Surface(s)	Answer Set	Answer Count	Surface Scope (per surface)	Persistence Notes
A	NR	FB	FB	Single	1	NR: single • FB: single	FB displays correct mark
B	NR	ST	ST	Single	1	NR: single • ST: single	ST displays correct mark
C	NR	TB	TB	Single	1	NR: single • TB: single	TB displays correct mark
D	NR	FB, ST	FB, ST	Single	1	NR: single • FB: single • ST: single	FB+ST display
E	NR	FB, TB	FB, TB	Single	1	NR: single • FB: single • TB: single	FB+TB display
F	NR	ST, TB	ST, TB	Single	1	NR: single • ST: single • TB: single	ST+TB display
G	NR	FB, ST, TB	FB, ST, TB	Single	1	NR: single • FB/ST/TB: single	All input surfaces display
H	FB	NR	NR	Single	1	FB: single • NR: single	NR displays
I	ST	NR	NR	Single	1	ST: single • NR: single	NR displays
J	TB	NR	NR	Single	1	TB: single • NR: single	NR displays
Notes:

Multi-surface Required Input means all listed surfaces must be satisfied for correctness (pitch-exact alignment where applicable).

Allowed Input is constrained to the same set as Required Input for A–G, and to NR for H–J, per locked intent.

JSON Schema (Preset Encoding)
Declarative schema only. No execution semantics.

{
  "$schema": "http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#",
  "title": "GLG Preset Definition",
  "type": "object",
  "required": [
    "id",
    "promptSurfaces",
    "requiredInputSurfaces",
    "allowedInputSurfaces",
    "answerRules",
    "surfaceScopes",
    "persistence"
  ],
  "properties": {
    "id": {
      "type": "string",
      "enum": ["A","B","C","D","E","F","G","H","I","J"]
    },
    "promptSurfaces": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": { "enum": ["noteRail","fretboard","staff","tab"] },
      "minItems": 1
    },
    "requiredInputSurfaces": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": { "enum": ["noteRail","fretboard","staff","tab"] },
      "minItems": 1
    },
    "allowedInputSurfaces": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": { "enum": ["noteRail","fretboard","staff","tab"] },
      "minItems": 1
    },
    "answerRules": {
      "type": "object",
      "required": ["answerSet","answerCount"],
      "properties": {
        "answerSet": {
          "type": "string",
          "enum": ["single","equivalent","octave","all"]
        },
        "answerCount": {
          "type": "integer",
          "minimum": 1
        }
      }
    },
    "surfaceScopes": {
      "type": "object",
      "additionalProperties": {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["scope"],
        "properties": {
          "scope": { "enum": ["single","rotating","all","random"] }
        }
      }
    },
    "persistence": {
      "type": "object",
      "additionalProperties": {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["displayAfterCorrect"],
        "properties": {
          "displayAfterCorrect": { "type": "boolean" }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
Validation notes
Presets A–J validate with answerSet: "single" and answerCount: 1.

Future expansion is schema-compatible by switching answerSet to "equivalent", "octave", or "all" and adjusting answerCount as specified, without changing preset identity.

Surface scope and persistence are explicit per surface, avoiding concept collapse.

Future Answer Sets Validation Matrix
Reminder: Answer Set ≠ Answer Count.

Answer Set defines the membership of valid targets (what counts as “the answer(s)”).

Answer Count defines how many submissions are required to complete the prompt.

Matrix (Single Notes context; extends cleanly later)
Answer Set	What “the answer” means	What a submission must satisfy	Default Answer Count	Notes / Edge rules
single	One specific instance (one target)	Submit exactly the designated instance on the required input surface(s)	1	In D–G, all required surfaces must match the same target pitch (and surface-specific representation).
Guitar_Learning_Game_Authoritat…

equivalent	All valid targets that produce the same pitch (same note + same octave) within the active domain	A submission is correct if it matches any one target in the equivalent set, and all required surfaces match that pitch exactly (including octave).	1	Canon already defines “equivalents” as same pitch within active fret range, subject to rule filters (spellings only when allowed).
Guitar_Learning_Game_Authoritat…

 This aligns with your locked “same staff note AND same octave.”
octave (future)	Same note name across a defined octave range (membership includes multiple octaves)	Correct if submission matches any one permitted octave target, unless Answer Count requires multiple. Multi-surface still demands internal agreement.	1	This differs from equivalent: equivalent is same pitch; octave is same name across octaves. When STAFF is required, octave acceptance must still obey absolute pitch checks once a target octave is chosen.
Guitar_Learning_Game_Authoritat…

all (future)	All instances of the same note name (unbounded or bounded by domain rules)	Correct if the required number of distinct instances (Answer Count) are submitted; each submission must be a valid member.	(see below)	Canon already uses “All” as a concept in reveal/visibility modes; “All” must remain distinct from “equivalent.”
Guitar_Learning_Game_Authoritat…

Answer Count behavior (paired with Answer Sets)
This is the mechanical contract we will encode (no assumptions beyond what’s needed for forward compatibility):

Answer Count = 1 (default)

single: one exact instance.

equivalent: any one of the equivalent targets (same pitch).

octave: any one of the allowed octaves.

all: any one instance (still valid; becomes meaningful when Answer Count > 1).

Answer Count > 1 (explicit exercise mode, later)

single: only valid if the prompt defines multiple distinct singles (rare; typically avoided).

equivalent: usually means “find N different fretboard locations that are equivalent” (same pitch), with uniqueness required per submission.

octave: “find N octaves” within the allowed range.

all: “find all instances,” where Answer Count is computed as the size of the membership set within the domain bounds.

Presets A–J compatibility check (no changes required)
Presets A–J remain structurally unchanged. Answer Sets only modify what counts as a correct target and how many are required.

D–G stay strict: all listed required surfaces must be correct simultaneously, regardless of Answer Set.
Guitar_Learning_Game_Authoritat…


Exact pitch enforcement remains mandatory when STAFF is part of required input, regardless of Answer Set selection.
Guitar_Learning_Game_Authoritat…


Minimal schema extension (optional but clean)
Your existing schema already supports this via:

answerRules.answerSet: "single" | "equivalent" | "octave" | "all"

answerRules.answerCount: integer

If we want to avoid ambiguity later, the only non-engine addition I recommend is a declarative octave bound for the octave / all sets (pure metadata, not logic), e.g.:

"answerRules": {
  "answerSet": "octave",
  "answerCount": 1,
  "octaveRange": { "min": 2, "max": 5 }
}
I will not add this unless you explicitly approve, because it introduces a new field (even though it is forward-compatible metadata).

If you want the next Phase B-2 artifact, I will produce a canon-ready “Preset + AnswerSet cross-table” showing, for each preset A–J, which Answer Sets are valid/meaningful and what their default Answer Count should be (still leaving all engine logic unwired).

{

  "type": "object",
  "required": [

      "type": "array",

    },
    "requiredInputSurfaces": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": { "enum": ["noteRail","fretboard","staff","tab"] },
      "minItems": 1
    },
    "allowedInputSurfaces": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": { "enum": ["noteRail","fretboard","staff","tab"] },
      "minItems": 1
    },

      "type": "object",
      "required": ["answerSet","answerCount"],

        "answerCount": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 1 },

          "type": "object",
          "required": ["min","max"],

          "then": { "required": ["octaveRange"] }

        {
          "if": { "properties": { "octaveRange": { "type": "object" } }, "required": ["octaveRange"] },
          "then": {
            "properties": {
              "octaveRange": {
                "properties": {
                  "max": { "type": "integer" }
                }
              }
            }
          }
        }
      ]
    },

    "surfaceScopes": {
      "type": "object",
      "additionalProperties": {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["scope"],
        "properties": { "scope": { "enum": ["single","rotating","all","random"] } },
        "additionalProperties": false
      }
    },

    "persistence": {
      "type": "object",
      "additionalProperties": {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["displayAfterCorrect"],
        "properties": { "displayAfterCorrect": { "type": "boolean" } },
        "additionalProperties": false
      }
    }
  },
  "additionalProperties": false
}
2) Canon rule encoded by schema
If answerSet === "octave", then octaveRange is required.

For other Answer Sets, octaveRange is not required (and should be omitted for clarity).

Example Answer Rules Block (as approved)

  "answerSet": "octave",
  "answerCount": 1,
  "octaveRange": { "min": 1, "max": 5 }

Instrument variability safeguard (no assumptions)
To keep future custom instruments safe without inventing behavior:

Schema contract: octaveRange is explicit whenever answerSet: "octave" is used.

Engine contract (later, not implemented now): instrument definitions may constrain, override, or validate octaveRange, but the preset data remains declarative.

This avoids hardcoding “guitar-only” assumptions while still letting you define octave-bounded exercises today.

You said:
one more thing to consider, if a teacher wanted a student to find 3 C notes anywhere on the fretboard do we have the system flexible enough to allow that?
ChatGPT said:
Yes — with what we have now, the system is already structurally flexible enough, but it requires one small, declarative addition to avoid ambiguity: a uniqueness rule for multi-answer tasks.

Why it works today (mostly)
A teacher’s request “find 3 C notes anywhere on the fretboard” maps cleanly to:

Surface: fretboard required/allowed input

Answer Set: not “equivalent” (that would be same pitch+octave), but either

octave (C across a defined octave range), or

all (all C instances in the domain)

Answer Count: 3

So the data model can already express the intent:

"answerRules": {
  "answerSet": "octave",
  "answerCount": 3,
  "octaveRange": { "min": 1, "max": 5 }
}
or

"answerRules": {
  "answerSet": "all",
  "answerCount": 3
}
The missing piece: preventing “same C three times”
To make “find 3” mean three distinct locations, we need to specify whether repeats are allowed. Otherwise a student could click the same C repeatedly and technically satisfy “3 submissions.”

Canon-safe, non-engine addition
Add one field under answerRules:

distinctTargets: boolean

true = each counted answer must be a different target instance (unique fret/string pair on fretboard, unique staff position, etc. depending on surface)

false = repeats allowed (rarely useful educationally)

Example (teacher’s case):

"answerRules": {
  "answerSet": "octave",
  "answerCount": 3,
  "octaveRange": { "min": 1, "max": 5 },
  "distinctTargets": true
}
Why this does not violate your locked definitions
We are not redefining Answer Sets or Answer Count.

We are adding a constraint on counting, not changing membership.

It is purely declarative metadata (Phase B-2 compliant), not engine logic.

Recommendation (tight and future-proof)
Default distinctTargets = true whenever answerCount > 1 unless explicitly overridden.

Keep it optional in schema, but strongly recommended for multi-answer teacher drills.

If you approve, I will:

extend the schema with distinctTargets (optional boolean), and

add a short canon note: “When Answer Count > 1, distinctTargets controls whether repeats are allowed.”

ChatGPT said:

Acknowledged. We will add distinctTargets as a declarative counting constraint under answerRules, enabling teacher drills like “find 3 C notes anywhere on the fretboard” without redefining Answer Sets.

Phase B-2 Update — Schema Extension (distinctTargets)
1) answerRules now supports multi-answer uniqueness
Add optional:

distinctTargets (boolean)

true = counted answers must be unique targets (prevents “same spot 3 times”)

false = repeats allowed

2) Updated answerRules schema (drop-in replacement)
"answerRules": {
  "type": "object",
  "required": ["answerSet","answerCount"],
  "properties": {
    "answerSet": { "type": "string", "enum": ["single","equivalent","octave","all"] },
    "answerCount": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 1 },

    "octaveRange": {
      "type": "object",
      "required": ["min","max"],
      "properties": {
        "min": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 0 },
        "max": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 0 }
      },
      "additionalProperties": false
    },

    "distinctTargets": { "type": "boolean" }
  },
  "additionalProperties": false,
  "allOf": [
    {
      "if": { "properties": { "answerSet": { "const": "octave" } }, "required": ["answerSet"] },
      "then": { "required": ["octaveRange"] }
    }
  ]
}
Canon behavior encoded (data-level only):

answerSet: "octave" ⇒ octaveRange required.

distinctTargets is optional (but enables the teacher drill cleanly).

Teacher drill example — “Find 3 C notes anywhere on the fretboard”
This is now expressible as:

"answerRules": {
  "answerSet": "octave",
  "answerCount": 3,
  "octaveRange": { "min": 1, "max": 5 },
  "distinctTargets": true
}
Alternate (also valid) if you later define “all” as bounded by domain/instrument rules:

"answerRules": {
  "answerSet": "all",
  "answerCount": 3,
  "distinctTargets": true
}
Canon note to record (Phase B-2)
“When Answer Count > 1, distinctTargets controls whether repeated selections of the same target instance are allowed to satisfy the count.”

Preset × Answer Set Compatibility Cross-Table (A–J)
Legend

✅ = valid + meaningful

⚠️ = valid but requires explicit fields (octaveRange, distinctTargets) or has a common classroom pitfall

⛔ = disallowed (conflicts with locked definitions / intent)

A–G: Note Rail → Board(s) (application + placement)
Preset	Prompt → Input	single	equivalent	octave	all
A	NR → FB	✅	✅	⚠️	⚠️
B	NR → ST	✅	✅	⚠️	⚠️
C	NR → TB	✅	✅	⚠️	⚠️
D	NR → FB+ST	✅	✅	⚠️	⚠️
E	NR → FB+TB	✅	✅	⚠️	⚠️
F	NR → ST+TB	✅	✅	⚠️	⚠️
G	NR → FB+ST+TB	✅	✅	⚠️	⚠️
Notes (A–G):

In multi-surface presets (D–G), correctness remains multi-domain: all required surfaces must be satisfied for the submission to count. (This aligns with the project’s “Accuracy Requirement: Multi-Domain” definition.)
Guitar_Learning_Game_Authoritat…


octave and all are flagged ⚠️ because they typically need:

octaveRange when answerSet="octave"

distinctTargets=true when answerCount>1 to prevent repeats (teacher drill integrity)

H–J: Board → Note Rail (recognition direction)
Preset	Prompt → Input	single	equivalent	octave	all
H	FB → NR	✅	✅	⚠️	⚠️
I	ST → NR	✅	✅	⚠️	⚠️
J	TB → NR	✅	✅	⚠️	⚠️
Notes (H–J):

Persistence rule you locked stays intact: FB/ST/TB persist, NR does not (rail space + future interactive rail behavior).

octave / all again require explicit metadata for bounded/unique counting in multi-answer drills.

Required Metadata Rules (to make ⚠️ cases unambiguous)
If answerSet = "octave"
You must include:

"octaveRange": { "min": 1, "max": 5 }
If answerCount > 1 and the teacher intends “different locations”
You should include:

"distinctTargets": true
This is exactly what enables: “Find 3 C notes anywhere on the fretboard” without allowing the same C to be clicked three times.

Example: Teacher Drill (Preset A shape, but rule-driven)
(Conceptually: NR prompts “C”, student finds 3 C’s on FB, unique.)

{
  "id": "A",
  "promptSurfaces": ["noteRail"],
  "requiredInputSurfaces": ["fretboard"],
  "allowedInputSurfaces": ["fretboard"],
  "answerRules": {
    "answerSet": "octave",
    "answerCount": 3,
    "octaveRange": { "min": 1, "max": 5 },
    "distinctTargets": true
  },
  "surfaceScopes": {
    "noteRail": { "scope": "single" },
    "fretboard": { "scope": "all" }
  },
  "persistence": {
    "noteRail": { "displayAfterCorrect": false },
    "fretboard": { "displayAfterCorrect": true }
  }
}
(No new preset created; this is the existing preset structure with different answerRules + a surface scope change.)

You said:

ChatGPT said:
You’re exactly right — and this is the critical inflection point where the system either stays coherent long-term or collapses under implicit behavior.

I’ll lay this out cleanly and canon-safely.

The Core Issue (Stated Precisely)
When multiple surfaces share the same role (Prompt or Required Input), there are two different axes of behavior that must be independently controlled:

Surface Participation
Which surfaces are involved at all?

Surface Coordination Mode
How those involved surfaces behave relative to each other

Without separating these, the UI will always imply rules that the engine does not formally own.

Required Conceptual Separation (Canon-Level)
We must introduce — explicitly — a Surface Coordination Layer.

This is not a new feature.
It is the missing formalization of behavior you have already described.

Layer Stack (Correct Order)
Surface Enabled

Surface Role

Prompt

Required Input

Allowed Input

Surface Coordination Mode ← newly formalized

Surface Scope

single / rotating / all / random

Persistence

Each layer answers a different question and must not be collapsed.

Surface Coordination Mode (Formal Definition)
This control only appears when 2+ surfaces share the same role.

Coordination Modes (Canonical Set)
These apply independently to Prompt and Required Input roles.

1. simultaneous
All selected surfaces act at the same time

Example:

Prompt appears on FB + ST together

Student must respond to both

Default for Required Input when multi-surface correctness is expected

2. rotating
One surface at a time

System cycles through enabled surfaces

Example:

Prompt appears on FB, then ST, then TAB

Deterministic sequence

3. random
One surface selected per task

Selection is non-deterministic

Example:

Prompt might appear on any enabled surface

4. single-source (Prompt-only)
Multiple surfaces enabled, but exactly one is the authoritative prompt source

Others may display contextually but do not generate prompts

This is especially important for avoiding double prompting chaos.

Why This Cannot Be Inferred
Consider just one ambiguous case without this layer:

Prompt Surfaces: FB + ST
Scope: single

This does not answer:

Are both prompting?

Is only one prompting?

If one, how is it chosen?

If both, is that simultaneous or alternating?

Your instinct is correct: this ambiguity explodes combinatorially as complexity grows.

UI Implication (Clean, Scalable)
This confirms your earlier conclusion: the Advanced Menu must expand.

Correct UI Pattern (Conceptual)
Step 1 — Select Surfaces
Checkbox list:

Note Rail

Fretboard

Staff

TAB

Step 2 — Assign Role(s)
Per selected surface:

Prompt

Required Input

Allowed Input

Step 3 — Coordination Controls (Contextual)
Appears only if multiple surfaces share a role.

Example:

Prompt Coordination

( ) Simultaneous

( ) Rotating

( ) Random

( ) Single Source → [dropdown: choose surface]

Same structure for Required Input Coordination.

This avoids clutter while remaining explicit.

When multiple surfaces are assigned the same role, the system must expose an explicit coordination mode.

1. Required Input vs Allowed Input (They Are Not Redundant — but only if defined precisely)
Canon-correct distinction
These answer different questions and must remain separate.

Required Input
Which surface(s) must be satisfied for the answer to be considered correct?

If a surface is Required:

It must receive a correct mark

Omission = incorrect / incomplete answer

Used to enforce multi-domain mastery

Example:

Preset D: Fretboard and Staff are required

Both must be correct simultaneously

Allowed Input
Which surface(s) may accept input without invalidating the attempt?

If a surface is Allowed but not Required:

Player may interact with it

It does not count toward correctness

It does not penalize the player

Used for:

Exploration

Cross-checking

Reducing frustration

Example:

Fretboard required

Staff allowed (student can click staff to think, but only FB is graded)

Why both are necessary
Without this split:

Any extra interaction risks becoming a penalty, or

Every enabled surface must be graded, which breaks learning flow

This distinction is especially important for educational-first behavior.

You are correct:
I drifted into generic language. We must realign to canon terms.

Canon terms (restated correctly)
Canon Term	Meaning
Mark	The act of placing an answer on a surface
Marked	A surface that can accept player marks
SAM (Show After Mark)	Whether a correct mark persists visually
Persistence	The result of SAM behavior (not a separate concept)
Correction to my earlier wording
❌ “Required Input / Allowed Input / Persistence” (as I framed it)

✅ Surface Roles must be expressed in canon terms

We should be using:

Surface Roles (Canon-Correct)
Each surface independently supports:

Prompt

Generates the question

Marked

Accepts player input

Required

Mark must be correct to satisfy the answer

SAM

Show After Mark (visual persistence)

Persistence is not its own toggle
It is the outcome of SAM = true

Required (on/off)

Prompt does not imply Marked

Coordination is role-scoped, not surface-scoped.

SAM does not require coordination.

Advanced Menu — Surface Controls Layout Spec (v1)
1. Purpose & Scope
The Advanced Menu exists to configure Surface Controls explicitly without locking behavior to presets. It must expose all relevant controls clearly and simultaneously to prevent implicit rules.

In scope

Surface Controls: Prompt / Mark / Persistence / SAM

Surface Cardinality

Multi-surface configuration clarity

Out of scope

Engine logic

Visual styling

Preset selection

New concepts or roles

2. Structural Requirement
2.1 Expanded Configuration Wing
The Advanced Menu must expand beyond standard flyouts into a wide configuration wing when Surface Controls are active.

Rationale: single-column layouts cannot safely represent multi-surface state.

This is a layout requirement, not a stylistic preference.

3. Layout Model (Conceptual)
Column 1 — Surface Index (Selection Context)
Purpose: establish which surface is being configured.

List all available surfaces:

Note Rail

Fretboard

Staff

TAB

Selecting a surface scopes configuration in Column 2.

Multiple surfaces may be selected to:

Compare settings

Apply batch edits (if supported later)

Rules

Selection does not imply activation.

No controls are changed at this stage.

Column 2 — Surface Controls (Per-Surface)
Purpose: configure exactly one surface at a time, explicitly.

For the selected surface, expose:

A. Core Controls
Prompt (toggle)

Mark (toggle)

Persistence (toggle)

SAM (toggle)

Constraints (enforced conceptually)

SAM is meaningful only if Mark = on

Persistence is meaningful only if Mark = on

Prompt does not imply Mark

Mark does not imply Prompt

These constraints must be visibly communicated, not hidden.

B. Cardinality Control
Single-select control:

single

equivalent

octave

all

Notes:

Cardinality is per surface

Cardinality affects how marks are interpreted, not whether marking is allowed

Column 3 — Contextual Guidance (Optional but Recommended)
Purpose: reduce misconfiguration without adding logic.

Display:

Short, static descriptions of each control

Warnings when configurations are uncommon (not forbidden)

No automation

No hidden rule changes

This column may be omitted if space is constrained, but the guidance must exist somewhere persistent.

4. Multi-Surface Configuration Rules
4.1 No Implicit Coordination
When multiple surfaces have Prompt = on or Mark = on, the system must not assume behavior.

The Advanced Menu must make it obvious that:

Multiple surfaces are active

Each surface is independently configured

Coordination behavior is governed by existing canon logic (simultaneous correctness in D–G) and not inferred by UI layout.

4.2 Preset Interaction
Presets initialize Surface Control states.

The Advanced Menu always reflects current state, not preset intent.

Any change in Advanced overrides preset defaults.

5. Error Prevention Principles (Canon-Safe)
The layout must prevent:

Hidden dependencies (e.g., Prompt implying Mark)

Vertical scrolling that hides related controls

Ambiguous multi-surface states

The layout must enable:

Side-by-side reasoning

Explicit configuration

Reversible changes

6. Non-Goals (Explicit)
The Advanced Menu must not:

Introduce new surface roles

Lock controls based on presets

Collapse multiple concepts into a single toggle

Infer educational intent

7. Canon Lock Statement
The Advanced Menu must provide sufficient spatial layout to expose all Surface Controls (Prompt, Mark, Persistence, SAM, Cardinality) without inference or hidden behavior.
Any layout that obscures these relationships is non-compliant.

8. Status
Conceptual spec complete

Terminology aligned to current canon

Ready to be referenced by:

Preset Pack v2

Menu architecture work

Future UI implementation

If you want, the next logical artifact would be a Surface Controls State Matrix (truth table style) to validate allowed vs discouraged combinations without adding new rules.

1. First: One Critical Correction (Very Important)
You repeatedly wrote “Prompt Coordination” for Mark, Persistence, and SAM.

Locked. Here is the audit resolution based on what you just set, plus the one remaining clarification (Pitch Framework).

Section B audit resolution
Intermission
Intermission settings are NOT included in Advanced.

Intermission is handled by the Intermission GUI only.

Game Timer start condition
You’re identifying a real missing spec detail:

Game Timer must not start until the Intermission GUI is closed.

If the GUI is open at match start, the timer remains paused until closure.

This belongs in Timer rules (Section B) as a start gate, not a menu setting.

Check Clock naming
Yes — we’ll treat these as the same thing in UI language:

Check Clock (AKA: Chess Clock / Time Bank)

And we only expose the chess-clock behavior:

per-player total time

time deducted per turn

C1 (turn window behavior / timing)
Acknowledged: you’re saying this is already addressed and canon will be adjusted accordingly. I will not reintroduce it.

C2 terminology
Confirmed:

Surface Scope is the correct term (not “Coordination”)

So the submenus will be labeled Surface Scope, not Coordination.

Pitch Framework question
You’re right to challenge this, because the term is causing drift.

What I meant by “Pitch Framework”
I was referring to the already-canon idea of selecting:

key / mode / scale constraints

accidentals behavior

chromatic vs constrained practice sets

In other words: the pitch-selection context that controls which notes are eligible to be prompted/marked.

Why it matters (the benefit)
Surface Controls decide where prompts/marks happen.
You still need something to decide what pitches are eligible.

That eligibility set is what you’ve been calling things like:

“Key”

“Modes (7 modes + chromatic + pentatonic + blues)”

“Accidentals by key; if no key selected all notes visible (wide open/chromatic)”

So the purpose is:

control the pool of notes/chords/scales the system draws from

support practice like “C major + A minor” or “blues + pentatonic” without rewriting presets

The fix (so we stop drifting)
We should stop using the term “Pitch Framework” entirely.

Instead we use a canon-friendly label such as:

Pitch Sets (recommended)

Key/Scale/Mode selectors

Accidentals tied per set (your requirement)

Add/Remove sets (your additive model)

If you do not want additive sets, we can keep it single-select (Key + Mode), but your own requirement about “test yourself on 2 or 3 or more areas” implies additive sets are intended.

Bottom line: this isn’t an extra system; it’s the name I used for the existing “what notes are in play” selector. I’ll rename it to Pitch Sets and keep it constrained to what you’ve already described.

Audit Resolution — Final Findings (Before Lock)
1. Pitch Sets — Placement & Scope (Resolved)
You’re right on all counts:

Pitch Sets are:

Highly relevant to chords, scales, intervals, arpeggios

Marginal for single-note practice

Placing them at the bottom of Advanced falsely implies:

low importance, or

global, always-on relevance

Correct placement
Pitch Sets should live adjacent to Surface Controls, because:

Surface Controls define where interaction happens

Pitch Sets define what musical content is eligible

For higher-order contexts (chords, scales, intervals, arpeggios), these two are inseparable

So structurally:

Surface Controls + Pitch Sets = Musical Interaction Definition

That pairing is canonical and intuitive.

2. Terminology Lock
❌ “Pitch Framework” — retired (caused confusion)

✅ Pitch Sets — accepted and accurate

This aligns with:

Additive model

Educational intent

Future expansion without semantic drift

3. Context Sensitivity (Important but Non-blocking)
You correctly noted:

Pitch Sets matter much more for:

Chords

Scales

Intervals

Arpeggios

Less so for:

Single-note drills

This does not require new controls now.

Instead, it implies:

Pitch Sets may be ignored or simplified when the active learning context is Single Note

UI can later gate or gray them out contextually

This is a presentation rule, not a system rule — safe to defer.

{
  "version": "v9",
  "surfaceControls": {
    "presets": "A|B|C|D|E|F|G|H|I|J",
    "pitchSets": [
      {
        "type": "keyScaleMode",
        "id": "C_major | A_minor | D_blues | ...",
        "accidentals": { "naturals": true, "sharps": false, "flats": false }
      }
    ],
    "surfaces": {
      "fretboard": { "$ref": "#/defs/surfaceBlock" },
      "staff": { "$ref": "#/defs/surfaceBlock" },
      "tab": { "$ref": "#/defs/surfaceBlock" },
      "noteRail": { "$ref": "#/defs/surfaceBlock" }
    }
  },

  "timing": {
    "turnTimer": { "enabled": true, "secondsPerTurn": 10 },
    "turnWindowBehavior": "fixed|rolling|hardCutoff",
    "multiAnswerPerTurn": false,

    "gameTimer": {
      "enabled": false,
      "totalSeconds": 300,
      "startGate": "afterIntermissionGuiClosed"
    },

    "checkClock": {
      "enabled": false,
      "aka": ["chessClock", "timeBank"],
      "secondsPerPlayer": 300,
      "deduct": "perTurn"
    },

    "runtimeCap": { "enabled": false, "maxTotalSeconds": 1800 },
    "increments": { "enabled": false, "secondsToAdd": 1 },
    "timeAddSubtract": {
      "enabled": false,
      "addSecondsOnCorrect": 1,
      "subtractSecondsOnMiss": 1
    },
    "timePenalties": { "enabled": false, "secondsToSubtract": 1 }
  },

  "penaltiesAccuracy": {
    "accuracyRequirement": "singleDomain|multiDomain",
    "accuracyScope": "perAnswer|perTurn",
    "penaltyTriggers": ["incorrectMark", "timeout", "incompleteAnswerSet"],
    "penaltyEffects": ["scorePenalty", "timePenalty", "turnLoss"]
  },

  "boards": {
    "mode": "shared|individual",
    "resetBehavior": "perTurn|perRound|perMatch|manual"
  },

  "defs": {
    "surfaceBlock": {
      "prompt": { "$ref": "#/defs/roleWithScope" },
      "mark": { "$ref": "#/defs/roleWithScope" },
      "persistence": { "$ref": "#/defs/role" },
      "sam": { "$ref": "#/defs/role" }
    },
    "role": {
      "enabled": true,
      "cardinality": { "$ref": "#/defs/cardinality" }
    },
    "roleWithScope": {
      "enabled": true,
      "cardinality": { "$ref": "#/defs/cardinality" },
      "surfaceScope": "single|rotating|all|random"
    },
    "cardinality": {
      "answerSet": "single|equivalent|octave|all",
      "answerCount": 1,
      "octaveRange": { "min": 1, "max": 5 }
    }
  }
}

answerSet = octave ⇒ requires octaveRange

Answer Set ≠ Answer Count (both exist independently)

Roles are strictly: Prompt / Mark / Persistence / SAM

Multi-surface behavior term is strictly: Surface Scope

UI Layout Implementation Plan v9

1) Panel Architecture
1.1 Entry + Expansion

Left column: Section Navigator
Musical Interaction

Time & Turn Flow

Penalties & Accuracy

remove button

Below is a revised UI Layout Implementation Plan (v9.1) that eliminates scrolling entirely under normal operation and ensures nothing shifts position when toggled.

UI Layout Implementation Plan v9.1
Revision focus: No scrolling · Fixed spatial layout · Stable control positions

1) Core Layout Principle (Locked)
No-scroll, fixed-grid design
The Advanced Menu must fit entirely within the available viewport at the target resolution.

No vertical or horizontal scrollbars under standard configurations.

Controls do not reflow, collapse, or move when enabled/disabled.

This is achieved through:

Fixed-height sections

Submenus rendered as overlays/drawers, not inline expansions

Strategic use of columns and grids

2) Panel Architecture (Revised)
2.1 Advanced Menu Container
Opens as a wide, fixed-dimension wing

Uses a static grid layout

Height is fixed relative to viewport (e.g., 85–90% of usable height)

Internal content never exceeds container height

2.2 Two-Column Primary Structure (No Scroll)
┌───────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ SECTION NAV                   │ ACTIVE SECTION CONTENT                     │
│ (Fixed)                       │ (Fixed Grid)                               │
├───────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Musical Interaction           │ Surface Controls  | Pitch Sets             │
│ Time & Turn Flow              │ (Fixed Pane)      | (Fixed Pane)           │
│ Penalties & Accuracy          │                   |                         │
│ Boards                        │                   |                         │
└───────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┘
Left column: fixed-width section selector

Right column: fixed layout per section

No section causes layout resizing

3) Musical Interaction (Most Complex Section)
3.1 Fixed Two-Pane Grid (No Scroll)
Pane A — Surface Controls
Fixed-height surface blocks:

Fretboard

Staff

TAB

Note Rail

Each surface block:

Proceeding ✅ — below is the Progressive Wing Layout (PWL) Contract Spec: states, transitions, constraints, and width tiers. This is layout-only (no engine behavior, no new mechanics).

Progressive Wing Layout Contract v1.0
Purpose: Provide a zero-scroll, zero-jitter, responsive Advanced Menu system that can grow in complexity without reflowing controls.

Canon name
Progressive Wing Layout (PWL)

1) Layout Regions
The UI is partitioned into fixed regions with controlled expansion:

Left Rails Stack (collapsed section rails)

Active Wing Panel (expanded section content)

Right Rail (Optional: Utility/Info rail — can exist but must not trigger scroll)

Playfield (main game area; must not reflow vertically)

Invariant: Expansion consumes horizontal space only.

2) PWL States
PWL operates as a finite set of stable states.

State S0 — Collapsed
Only a thin Main Menu rail is visible.

Advanced content is hidden.

No scrollbars anywhere.

Exit: open Advanced → S1

State S1 — Advanced Open (Section Navigator)
Advanced wing expands to a defined base width.

Shows section navigator + a default landing section.

Other sections appear as rails or tabs but do not move.

Exit: select a section → S2
Exit: close Advanced → S0

State S2 — Section Focus
One section becomes active (e.g., Musical Interaction).

Inactive sections compress into labeled rails.

Active section panel gains more width (Tiered).

Exit: select different section → S2 (same state, different active section)
Exit: close Advanced → S0
Exit: open submenu → S3

State S3 — Submenu Open (Drawer/Popover)
A submenu opens as a lateral overlay inside the Advanced wing.

Parent section does not resize; no inline expansion.

Underlay remains visible and stationary.

Exit: close submenu → S2

3) Transitions (Allowed Only)
Transitions must be deterministic and never cause vertical reflow.

From	To	Trigger
S0	S1	Open Advanced
S1	S2	Select a section
S2	S3	Open a submenu (Cardinality / Surface Scope)
S3	S2	Close submenu
S1/S2/S3	S0	Collapse Advanced
Forbidden transitions

Any transition that introduces scrollbars

Any transition that changes control positions within a panel

4) Hard Constraints (Non-negotiable)
These are the “no drift” UX laws.

No scrolling in Advanced wing (no vertical scrollbars)

No inline expansion (enabling options never changes row height)

No reflow (controls never shift position when toggled)

Horizontal compression only (panels collapse sideways)

Disabled controls remain visible (never hidden; use disabled + explanation)

Submenus overlay (drawer/popover), never expand parent

Position memory preserved (returning to a section restores prior focus state)

5) Width Tiers (Responsiveness Without Reflow)
Use fixed tiers with breakpoints (numbers are illustrative; the concept is the contract).

Tier definitions
Rail (R): 44–64px (label rotated or stacked text)

Narrow Panel (N): 280–360px

Standard Panel (S): 420–560px

Wide Panel (W): 640–840px

Max Panel (X): 900–1100px (if viewport allows)

Tier allocation by state
S0: Main rail only (R)

S1: Section nav (N) + content (S)

S2 (Musical Interaction): content (W or X), inactive sections as rails (R)

S2 (Time/Turn, Penalties, Boards): content (S or W), inactive rails (R)

S3: active content keeps its tier; submenu consumes an overlay slice (e.g., 320–420px) from within the wing.

Rule: If the viewport cannot fit the target tier, reduce the number of visible rails (not by adding scroll). Rails can collapse into a single “Sections” rail that opens a chooser overlay.

6) Section Layout Contracts (Fixed Grids)
Each section has a fixed internal grid to prevent jitter.

Musical Interaction (highest density)
Two fixed panes:

Pane A: Surface Controls grid (fixed rows for 4 surfaces × 4 roles)

Pane B: Pitch Sets list (bounded visible rows; add disabled when full)

No expansions: Cardinality / Surface Scope open as S3 overlay.

Time & Turn Flow
Fixed card slots (pre-allocated):

Turn Timer

Turn Behavior

Multi-answer toggle

Game Timer (with start-gate note)

Check Clock (AKA list)

Runtime Cap

Increments

Time add/subtract

Time penalties

Cards toggle enabled/disabled but never resize.

Penalties & Accuracy
Two fixed cards:

Accuracy requirement + scope

Triggers + effects

Boards
Single fixed card:

Shared/Individual

Reset behavior

7) Submenu Contracts
Cardinality submenu (S3 overlay)
Answer Set radios

Answer Count stepper

Octave Range steppers shown only if answerSet=octave

Static explanation block

Close button

Must not affect any underlay position.

Surface Scope submenu (S3 overlay)
single / rotating / all / random radios

Static explanation block

Close button

8) Failure-Mode Rules (No Scroll Enforcement)
If content threatens to exceed space, the system must respond by constraining UI, not adding scroll.

Allowed mitigations:

Cap visible Pitch Sets rows; disable “Add” after cap

Collapse inactive sections into a single “Sections” rail

Use overlays for selection lists (e.g., “Add Pitch Set” opens chooser overlay)

Use compact label treatments (icon+short label) while retaining clarity

Forbidden mitigations:

Introducing scrollbars

Wrapping controls into new rows

Collapsing sections into hidden accordions that change height

9) Implementation Notes (Layout-only)
Use a CSS grid / flex structure with explicit widths for tiers

Store UI state:

activeSection

submenuOpen (none/cardinality/scope)

submenuContext (surface + role)

pitchSetCount (for cap + disabling Add)

Prefer transitions with constant dimensions (opacity/translate) rather than resizing.

PWL Overlay Contract Addendum v1.0
1) Anchor Rule
Playfield never participates in layout calculations for the menu system.

The menu system is an overlay layer above the playfield.

Invariant: Activating any overlay never changes playfield size, position, or reflow.

3.1 Rails Overlay
Default: collapsed rails appear along the left edge as slim vertical tabs.

Rails do not reserve space; they float over the playfield.

3.2 Wing Overlay (Advanced)
Opens from the left edge and expands inward.

Width is controlled by PWL tiers (N/S/W/X) but bounded by viewport.

The wing is clipped to its own container. No scrollbars.

3.3 Submenu Overlay
Opens inside the wing as a right-side drawer.

Does not resize or shift any content behind it.

Dismiss returns to the exact prior state (position memory preserved).

4) Input & Interaction Rules (Layout-level)
Clicking the playfield while the wing is open:

either does nothing, or

closes only submenus (S3 → S2), depending on your preference later

Wing overlay captures pointer events within its bounds.

Playfield remains interactive outside the wing bounds (unless you later choose a “modal overlay” behavior).

No decisions needed now; these are implementation toggles.

ChatGPT said:
You’re exactly right to stop here. This is the correct choke point in the design, and you’ve articulated the reason precisely:

Pitch Sets are doing conceptual work that is currently split, implicit, and under-defined.
Until Pitch Sets are flushed out, the Advanced Menu cannot be finalized without risk of drift or retrofits.

Below is a clean, canon-safe reframing of Pitch Sets, followed by a systematic audit and expansion plan that stays aligned with everything already designed.

No invention. No UI guesswork. Just structure.

1. What Pitch Sets Actually Are (Clarified)
Pitch Sets are not just “key / scale selectors.”

They are:

The authoritative definition of what pitches are eligible to be generated or accepted, independent of where or how they are shown.

This makes Pitch Sets the bridge between:

Musical theory logic

Surface Controls (Prompt / Mark / Persistence / SAM)

Game rules (timers, answer count, penalties)

That is why this belongs in Advanced, not Basic.

2. Why Pitch Sets Currently Feel Redundant
Right now, Basic Menu options implicitly do three things:

Choose a tonal context (key, scale, mode)

Restrict pitch generation

Affect accidentals and expected answers

But:

These rules are implicit

They do not scale to chords, intervals, or progressions

They are not composable (you can’t add/remove multiple sets cleanly)

Pitch Sets formalize this logic so:

Basic Menu becomes a preset shortcut

Advanced Menu becomes the truth layer

That’s the correct hierarchy.

3. Canon Definition (Proposed, Needs Your Confirmation)
Before listing options, this definition should be locked or corrected:

Pitch Set
A Pitch Set defines a bounded collection of pitch identities (notes, pitch classes, or pitch groupings) that the game may use for prompting, validation, and scoring.

If you agree, everything below follows cleanly.
If not, we adjust the definition first.

4. Pitch Set Categories (No UI Yet, Just System Scope)
Pitch Sets must support five musical domains you already intend to support:

A. Single Notes
B. Intervals
C. Chords
D. Scales / Modes
E. Arpeggios & Progressions
Each category has shared structure, but different parameters.

5. Shared Pitch Set Properties (All Categories)
Every Pitch Set, regardless of type, needs:

Core Identity
Type: note | interval | chord | scale | arpeggio | progression

Root / Reference (where applicable)

Naming / Label (for UI clarity, not logic)

Scope Controls
Accidental policy (derived or explicit)

Octave constraints (min / max or derived)

Answer eligibility (ties into Answer Set logic)

Lifecycle
Enabled / disabled

Additive (can coexist with other Pitch Sets)

Removable without reflow

This matches the slot map you already approved.

6. Category-Specific Requirements (Audit)
A. Single Note Pitch Sets
These mirror current Basic behavior but explicitly.

Required properties:

Note set (e.g., C, D, E…)

Accidentals (natural / sharp / flat)

Octave range (optional or derived)

Chromatic vs constrained

This is what you already have — now formalized.

B. Interval Pitch Sets
Already discussed previously, but not formalized.

Required properties:

Root note or dynamic root

Interval types (m2, M2, m3, etc.)

Direction (ascending / descending / both)

Harmonic vs melodic

Compound interval allowance (future-safe)

These must live in Pitch Sets, not Surface Controls.

C. Chord Pitch Sets
This is where Pitch Sets become essential.

Required properties:

Chord quality (maj, min, dim, aug, etc.)

Extensions (7, 9, 11, 13)

Inversions (root, 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th)

Voicing rules (tight, open, drop — future)

Root policy (fixed / diatonic / any)

These cannot be expressed via Basic Menu cleanly.

D. Scale / Mode Pitch Sets
Currently conflated with “key.”

Required properties:

Scale or mode type

Root

Mode degree context (Ionian, Dorian, etc.)

Ascending / descending behavior

Diatonic enforcement

This replaces “Pitch Framework” confusion with Pitch Set clarity.

E. Arpeggios & Progressions
Most advanced, but already anticipated.

Required properties:

Base chord or scale reference

Pattern (1–3–5, etc.)

Direction and traversal rules

Progression logic (static vs rotating)

This fits naturally once the above are locked.

7. Relationship to Surface Controls (Critical)
Pitch Sets answer WHAT pitches exist.
Surface Controls answer WHERE and HOW they are presented.

They must remain orthogonal.

This is why your instinct that “this is the right place” is correct.

8. What We Should Do Next (Before UI Lock)
Before touching the menu again, we should produce one document:

🎯 Pitch Set Canon Specification
Contents:

Locked definition

Category list (A–E)

Required fields per category

Explicit non-goals (what Pitch Sets do NOT control)

Mapping note: how Basic Menu presets map into Pitch Sets

No UI.
No schema yet.
Just truth.

Once that exists:

The Advanced Menu becomes inevitable

Slot counts can be validated

No future redesigns are required

My Recommendation

Draft the Pitch Set Canon Specification

Cross-check it against:

Existing Basic Menu behavior

Surface Controls

Answer Sets / Cardinality

Flag only true ambiguities for your decision

This is the correct sequencing, and you caught it at the right time.

First: Re-frame Pitch Sets (Important)
Pitch Sets should not mirror Basic controls.
They should only exist where Basic controls are insufficient, ambiguous, or non-composable.

So the guiding rule becomes:

If a concept can be fully and cleanly expressed in Basic, it does not belong in Pitch Sets unless Pitch Sets add new expressive power.

With that rule applied, here is the corrected model.

A. Single Notes — Do NOT need a Pitch Set category (Confirmed)
You are correct.

Basic controls already define:

Key / accidentals

Chromatic vs constrained

Fret range

There is no additional expressive depth for single notes yet.

Decision
✅ Single Notes are not a Pitch Set category.

Single-note behavior remains:

Defined by Basic Menu

Interpreted by Surface Controls + Answer Sets

Pitch Sets begin only when relationships between notes matter.

B. Intervals — Pitch Set Category (Refined)
You asked good questions here, so let’s clean this up.

What Intervals actually need
Intervals are about distance and reference, not harmony.

Pitch Set – Interval should define:

Required
Reference Type

Fixed root (e.g., always from C)

Relative (from current note, previous answer, or moving root)

Distance

Interval class (m2, M2, m3, etc.)

Direction:

Ascending

Descending

Either

Traversal Constraint

Fixed starting position (no movement)

Movable starting position (can slide)

Max fret distance (span constraint)

Clarifications you asked for
Harmonic vs melodic
❌ Remove. That belongs to chords/arpeggios, not interval recognition.

Compound interval allowance
This simply means:

Can the interval exceed an octave (9ths, 10ths, etc.)

This is optional and future-facing
✅ Keep as an advanced toggle, not default

Result
Intervals remain a Pitch Set, but narrowly and correctly scoped.

C. Chords — Pitch Set Category (Expanded, Corrected)
This is where Pitch Sets earn their place.

You identified the correct dimensions.

Required chord Pitch Set controls
Chord Quality

Major, minor, diminished, augmented, suspended, etc.

Inversions

Root, 1st, 2nd, 3rd (as applicable)

Voicing / Shape

Closed

Open

Barred

String Constraints

Allowed strings

Required string count

Span Constraints

Max fret distance

Root Policy

Fixed root

Diatonic root (ties into scale Pitch Sets)

Optional (advanced, but valid)
Diatonic chord enforcement
(Allowed only if a Scale Pitch Set is active)

This category is absolutely correct to live in Pitch Sets.

D. Scales / Modes — Pitch Set Category (But Narrower)
You’re right that there is overlap with Basic controls, so the rule is:

Basic defines the tonal context. Pitch Sets define traversal and testing rules.

What belongs here (not Basic)
Scale / mode selection (Ionian, Dorian, etc.)

Traversal behavior

Ascending only

Descending only

Ascend + descend

Degree restrictions

Specific degrees only

Skip patterns (e.g., thirds)

Octave traversal rules

Single octave

Multi-octave (bounded)

What does NOT belong here
Key selection

Accidentals (unless overridden intentionally)

So yes: overlap exists, but this is where depth lives, not redundancy.

E. Arpeggios vs Progressions — You are correct: split them
This is an important correction.

E1. Arpeggios — Pitch Set Category
Arpeggios are note-order logic, not harmony movement.

Required controls:

Source

From chord Pitch Set

From scale Pitch Set

Order

Root-first

Up

Down

Custom pattern

Direction

One-way

Bounce

Shape constraints

Fixed shape

Movable shape

Span constraints

Arpeggios stay in Pitch Sets.

E2. Progressions — NOT a Pitch Set
You are absolutely right.

Progressions are:

Chord-to-chord movement

Temporal / sequence logic

Often multiplayer- or lesson-driven

They should live as a separate Advanced section, not under Pitch Sets.

Progressions consume:

Chord Pitch Sets

Scale Pitch Sets

But they do not define pitches themselves.

✅ Progressions are their own system.

Corrected Pitch Set Categories (Final)
After your feedback, the clean, non-redundant list is:

Intervals

Chords

Scales / Modes

Arpeggios

❌ Single Notes — handled by Basic
❌ Progressions — separate system

This is lean, expandable, and future-proof.

Min/Max skipped strings

Min span

Max span

max fret − min fret within the shape

Chord Pitch Set v1 — Modes (Locked)
A Chord Pitch Set supports three selection modes:

Builder Mode
Generates eligible chords from selected qualities/extensions/alterations.

Bank Mode
Uses only an explicit curated list of chord recipes.

Hybrid Mode (Advanced)
Uses Bank + Builder together with a clear precedence rule.

Hybrid precedence (canon-safe, minimal)
Bank is always included

Builder-generated chords fill the remaining eligible pool

If a Bank entry conflicts with Builder constraints, Bank still wins
(This is the whole point of “I want maj chords, but also min7add9.”)

Chord Source Policy — “By Key” vs “By Root” (Locked)
Add a required selector for how chords derive their tonal reference:

Chord Source
By Key (Diatonic)

Chords are generated/validated as scale-degree chords

Requires an active key/scale context (Basic or Pitch Set scale context)

Diatonic enforcement is effectively inherent here

By Root

Chords are generated/validated from an explicit root note (or selection rule)

Can be diatonic or non-diatonic depending on enforcement toggle

Root selection options (within “By Root”)
Fixed Root (e.g., always C)

Rotating Root (cycles through allowed roots)

Random Root (from allowed root set)

Diatonic Root Pool (roots limited to current scale degrees)

(These are selection policies, not new mechanics; they match your existing scope vocabulary.)

Chord Builder ↔ Progressions mesh
Yes — with By Key (diatonic), your chord builder naturally becomes a degree generator (I, ii, iii, IV, V, vi, vii° plus allowed extensions/alterations), which is exactly what progressions want. This is the cleanest possible coupling:

Pitch Sets (Chords) define the allowed chord space

Progressions define the movement/order through that space

“Detect the key/scale when custom chords are added”
This is a good assist feature, and it can be added without inventing new musical rules by treating it as analysis + suggestion, not automation.

Where it belongs
Primary: Progressions (because the user is explicitly building a chord chain)
Secondary: Chord Bank entry (when adding a custom chord to the bank)

What it does (canon-safe)
When a user adds custom chords, the system computes candidate keys/scales and displays:

Top 3 likely keys (and modes/scales if enabled)

Confidence indicator (e.g., High / Medium / Low)

A list of “out-of-key tones” per candidate (so it’s explainable)

No forced changes. Just guidance.

Minimal logic (robust, explainable)
Convert each chord into a pitch-class set (12-tone membership)

Aggregate across the bank/progression into a weighted pitch-class profile

Compare to:

diatonic major/minor templates (and later modes)

Rank candidates by match score:

reward included diatonic tones

penalize accidentals outside the candidate set

If multiple candidates tie, show all ties (don’t guess)

Why this stays clean
It doesn’t alter gameplay logic

It doesn’t redefine what “diatonic” means

It helps teachers/players align their custom material to a coherent key

Locked ✅ — the Key/Scale Detection Assist Tool is now a required system feature (not merely “future”). It will apply to Chord Banks, Scale Sets, and Progressions, and it will explicitly support accepting suggestions via Add / Replace actions.

Below is the canon-ready spec (logic + UI actions), designed to be deterministic, explainable, and non-invasive.

Assist Tool Canon Spec v1
Name
Tonal Assist (Key/Scale Detection + Suggestion Actions)

Purpose
When a user adds or edits custom musical material, Tonal Assist:

analyzes the material,

generates candidate tonal contexts (key/scale/mode),

explains why,

lets the user apply a suggestion via Add or Replace.

This is used to build reliable libraries later.

1) Where Tonal Assist Runs
A) Chord Bank
Triggered on:

Add chord

Remove chord

Edit chord (quality/extensions/alterations/slash/root)

Analyzes:

the entire bank (not just the last chord) unless “Analyze last chord only” is explicitly selected later.

B) Scale Sets (custom scale builder)
Triggered on:

Add/remove a pitch degree

Add/remove chromatic tones

Change traversal pattern (optional later)

C) Progressions (custom progression builder)
Triggered on:

Add/remove chord step

Edit step

Reorder steps

2) Tonal Assist Outputs
2.1 Suggestions List (ranked)
Returns top N candidates (default 3):

Candidate Key/Scale/Mode label (e.g., C Major, A Minor, D Dorian)

Confidence: High / Medium / Low

“Why” summary:

matched tones count

out-of-set tones list (if any)

strongest chord-degree fits (for progressions)

2.2 Explainability Requirement (Locked)
Every suggestion must provide:

Included tones (intersection)

Excluded / conflicting tones (difference)

For chords: suggested roman-degree mapping if byKey is active

No black-box “trust me.”

3) Apply Actions: Add / Replace (Required)
Each suggestion must provide two actions:

3.1 Add
Adds the suggested tonal context without removing current settings.

Examples:

Add a new Pitch Set: “Add Scale Set: C Major”

Add a Key Context to the progression library metadata

Add a “Suggested Key” tag to a chord bank

Use when:

user wants multiple candidate contexts preserved

ambiguous sets exist

3.2 Replace
Replaces the currently active tonal context with the selected suggestion.

Examples:

Replace current scale/key setting with “A Minor”

Replace progression’s current tonal context

Use when:

user wants one authoritative context

player/teacher wants strictness

4) Canon Safety Rules (Non-negotiable)
Tonal Assist never changes settings automatically

Suggestions are non-binding until Add/Replace is pressed

If confidence is Low:

show “Ambiguous” first

encourage storing multiple via Add (still no coercion)

For Chord Source = By Key:

Assist results must prioritize diatonic keys that fit the bank/progression

but may still surface alternatives if ambiguity exists

5) Minimal Deterministic Logic (Robust Enough Now)
5.1 Representations
Convert chords/scales/progressions into a pitch-class set (12-tone membership)

Track tone frequency weights (optional, simple counts)

5.2 Candidate generation (v1)
Test against:

Major / Natural minor templates

(Modes can be added later without changing structure)

5.3 Scoring (v1)
Score = matches − penalties

reward matching diatonic tones

penalize out-of-set tones

Tie-handling:

show all ties at the same rank

avoid forced winner

6) Where it appears in the Advanced Menu (No-scroll compliant)
Tonal Assist is not a new main section. It appears as a Details overlay utility:

In Chord Bank overlay: “Analyze Tonality” button + suggestions panel

In Scale Builder overlay: “Analyze Tonality” button + suggestions

In Progression Builder overlay: “Analyze Tonality” button + suggestions

Each suggestion row has:

Label + confidence

“Why” expand (overlay or popover)

Add button

Replace button

No layout reflow.

Pitch Set Canon Specification v1
0) Canon Purpose
Pitch Sets define what musical material is eligible for prompting/marking/validation.
They do not define where/how it is displayed (Surface Controls) or when it is asked (Timers/Turn Flow).

1) Pitch Set Types in v1
Pitch Sets exist only where Basic controls are insufficient.

Included
Intervals

Chords

Scales / Modes

Arpeggios

Excluded
Single Notes (handled by Basic + Surface Controls)

Progressions (separate system; consumes Pitch Sets)

2) Universal Constraint Layer (applies to Chords / Scales-Modes / Arpeggios; optional for Intervals)
These fields exist because min/max alone is insufficient.

2.1 Strings (required)
Allowed Strings: individual toggles per string (e.g., 6–1)

Min Strings / Max Strings

Min Muted Strings / Max Muted Strings

Min Skipped Strings / Max Skipped Strings

Definitions:

Muted string = included in grip/shape but not sounded

Skipped string = not part of traversal/shape

2.2 Span (required)
Min Span / Max Span
Span definition: maxFret - minFret within the shape.

2.3 Notes per string (required for Scales/Arpeggios; optional for Chords)
Min Notes per String / Max Notes per String

2.4 Position / Movement (required)
Position Policy: fixed | movable

Movement Limit: bounded by Basic fret range (default), with optional max-shift cap

3) Tonal Enforcement Layer (required)
3.1 Diatonic enforcement (required across Pitch Sets where applicable)
Diatonic Enforcement: ON by default, exposed as a control

For specific cases (Chord Source = By Key) it becomes locked ON (see §4)

3.2 Chromatic builder (required for Scales/Modes)
Scales/Modes must support:

explicit chromatic inclusion (passing tones)

full custom pitch-class set (chromatic scale builder)

4) Chords Pitch Set v1 (Expanded)
Chords are constraint-driven with two selection families and an advanced hybrid.

4.1 Chord Mode (required)
Builder

Bank

Hybrid (advanced)

Hybrid rule (locked):

Bank always included

Bank overrides Builder conflicts

Builder fills remaining pool

4.2 Chord Source (required)
By Key (Diatonic)

By Root

By Key lock (locked)
If Chord Source = By Key:

Diatonic enforcement is ON and locked

Chords are interpreted as scale-degree chords within the active tonal context

By Root
Allowed Roots (set)

Root selection policy: fixed | rotating | random | diatonic root pool

4.3 Builder Inputs (required)
Quality family: maj, min, dim, aug, sus2, sus4

7th behavior: none | maj7 | min7 | dom7 | dim7 | half-diminished (ø7)

Extensions: add9/9, add11/11, 13

Alterations: ♭5/♯5, ♭9/♯9, ♭11/♯11, ♭13

Slash chords:

allow slash chords (on/off)

allowed bass degrees/tones

Inversions: allowed inversion set

Voicing types: barred | closed | open

Physical constraints: Universal Constraint Layer (strings/mutes/skips/span)

4.4 Bank Entries (required when Bank/Hybrid)
Each bank entry minimally stores:

source: byKey/byRoot alignment

root (or degree if byKey)

quality + 7th + extensions + alterations + slash + inversion constraints

4.5 “Chord construction UI”
Full chord construction/sequencing is Progressions-primary

Pitch Sets define allowed chord space; bank is curated membership, not a full progression tool

5) Scales / Modes Pitch Set v1 (Expanded)
5.1 Identity
scale/mode type (includes Ionian–Locrian + major/minor variants as applicable)

root policy (fixed/relative/byKey alignment)

5.2 Traversal & pattern (required)
direction: ascending | descending | both

pattern families: 2–3, 3–2, 2–2, custom

octave traversal bounds: single vs multi-octave (bounded)

5.3 Physical constraints (required)
universal constraint layer

notes-per-string (critical for 3NPS)

5.4 Degree filters (required)
include-only degrees (e.g., 1,3,5)

exclude degrees (e.g., avoid 4)

5.5 Chromatic builder (required)
allow passing tones

allow full custom pitch-class membership

6) Arpeggios Pitch Set v1 (Expanded)
6.1 Source (required)
derived from Chord Pitch Set

derived from Scale/Mode Pitch Set

6.2 Order & motion (required)
order: up | down | root-first | custom

motion: one-way | bounce | loop

6.3 Physical constraints (required)
universal constraint layer

notes-per-string

6.4 Termination rule (required)
stop at octave

stop at shape end

stop after N notes

6.5 Diatonic enforcement (required when derived from byKey contexts)
respects diatonic locks from its source contexts

7) Intervals Pitch Set v1 (Refined)
Intervals are distance + reference.

Required:

reference type: fixed root | relative/moving reference

interval class set (m2, M2, m3…)

direction: ascending | descending | either

start position policy: fixed | movable

optional: compound interval allowance (9ths+)

Excluded:

harmonic vs melodic (not an interval pitch-set concern)

Dependency Map
This is the authoritative interaction graph.

1) Pitch Sets → Surface Controls
Pitch Sets define eligible musical material (what)

Surface Controls define render + input behavior (where/how)

Surface Controls never redefine tonal eligibility.

2) Progressions ↔ Pitch Sets
Progressions consume:

Chord Pitch Sets (builder/bank/hybrid; byKey/byRoot)

Scale/Mode Pitch Sets (for degree context)

Progressions define:

chord movement/order

step sequencing

progression builder (explicit construction)

Rule: Progressions may build explicit chord sequences; Pitch Sets define the allowed pool.

3) Tonal Assist ↔ Builders and Gameplay
Tonal Assist reads:

chord banks

scale builders

progressions

live gameplay history (bounded window)

Tonal Assist suggests:

tonal contexts (key/scale/mode)

Tonal Assist writes only when user chooses:

Add → adds a tonal context to library or active set list

Replace → replaces current tonal context selection

4) Diatonic enforcement locks
If Chords are By Key:

diatonic enforcement is ON and locked

Scale/Mode pitch sets may enforce diatonic or allow chromatic builder depending on settings

Arpeggios inherit enforcement from their source context

5) Basic Settings relationship
Basic settings remain authoritative for single-note domain constraints (e.g., fret range, baseline key/mode selection)

Pitch Sets extend capability for complex domains (chords/scales/arpeggios/intervals)

No duplication unless Pitch Sets introduce additional expressive power.

Scale / Mode Pattern Families — Canon Refinement
Design Principle (Locked)

Canon Pattern Families (v1)

CAGED

C, A, G, E, D shapes

Fixed vs rotating position

Required for completeness and advanced theory.

NPS + Direction + Position rules

Where they live (explicitly)

Box / Position

Positions 1–5 are valid and selectable

Works for:

Why this is correct and complete
The Box / Position family was intentionally defined to cover:

Pentatonic box systems

Blues box systems

Modal box variants

Position indices (1–5) are first-class, not implicit

Rotation, fixing, and overlap rules apply cleanly

Physical constraints (strings, span, skips, mutes) still apply

Tonal Assist can analyze these shapes just like any other scale set

What we explicitly avoided (by design)
We did not hard-code “Pentatonic Shape 1–5” as a special-case system

They emerge naturally from:

Scale identity (pentatonic)

Pattern family (Box / Position)

Position index (1–5)

This keeps the system:

cleaner

more extensible

consistent with how CAGED, modal boxes, and other systems work

ChatGPT said:

Thought for 11s
Progressions System Design v1
Scope: Canon design + menu fit + data model. No engine wiring.
Placement: Advanced Menu → Musical Interaction → Progressions (separate from Pitch Sets), consistent with your earlier correction (progressions ≠ pitch sets). Uses the same PWL overlay pattern and no scrolling.

1) Canon Definition
A Progression is a time-ordered chord movement system that:

selects or constructs chords (primarily from your Chord Pitch Set),

defines movement rules (by key/degree or by root),

defines advancement rules (when/how steps change).

Progressions do not redefine Surface Controls (Prompt/Mark/Persistence/SAM). They only determine what chord comes next.

2) Inputs and Dependencies
Progressions consume:

Required inputs
Chord Pitch Set (Builder / Bank / Hybrid)

Chord Source policy (By Key vs By Root)

Optional inputs (recommended)
Scale/Mode Pitch Set (for By Key context, degrees, mode awareness)

Tonal Assist (for detection + Add/Replace of tonal context)

3) Progression Modes
A progression must select one primary mode:

Mode A — By Key (Diatonic Degrees) ✅ (recommended default)
Steps are expressed as degrees (I, ii, iii, IV, V, vi, vii°) plus permitted qualities/extensions.

Diatonic enforcement is ON and locked when progression is By Key (mirrors your chord By Key lock and keeps it beginner-safe).

Works perfectly with the chord builder.

Mode B — By Root (Explicit Root Movement)
Steps are expressed as root notes (or root-selection policies).

Can still optionally enforce diatonic roots via “Diatonic Root Pool,” but not locked by default.

4) Progression Builder (Required)
You requested this as a must; here’s the clean structure.

4.1 Step List (Fixed Row Cap, No Scroll)
Each step is a row with reserved slots:

Step Row Slots
Step index (1…N)

Step definition button (opens Step Details overlay)

Remove step

Move up/down (reorder)

Enabled toggle (optional)

4.2 Step Details Overlay (No Scroll)
Each step defines:

For By Key
Degree (I–vii°)

Quality constraints (optional override; otherwise inherit from Chord Pitch Set)

Extensions/alterations constraints (optional override)

Inversion/voicing constraints (optional override)

Slash chord allowance (optional override)

For By Root
Root note OR root-selection policy

Quality + extensions + alterations (or inherit)

Optional “restrict to chord bank only” toggle (advanced)

Rule: Overrides are allowed but should default to inherit from the Chord Pitch Set to keep it understandable.

5) Advancement Rules (Turn Behavior)
Progressions must specify when the next step occurs.

Advancement triggers (select one)
Per Turn

Per Correct Answer

Per N Correct Answers

Per Timer Tick / Time Interval (only if timer modes support it)

Manual (teacher-controlled / test mode)

Looping (required)
Loop: on/off

If on: loop back to step 1 after last step

Randomization (optional but clean)
Sequential (default)

Random step (bounded)

Weighted step (later)

6) Library & Reuse (Required for your “libraries later” goal)
Progressions must support:

Save progression to library

Load progression from library

Name + tags (mode, difficulty, genre)

This is necessary for:

teacher presets

curated lesson packs

progression packs that align with chord/scale libraries

7) Tonal Assist Integration (Locked Placement)
As you decided, Tonal Assist lives in the Gameplay Misc Information Panel.

For Progressions:

When building/editing a progression, Tonal Assist can analyze:

the step chord pool (resolved from chord builder/bank)

actual explicit bank/step chords (if present)

Tonal Assist must provide:

Suggestions (key + mode)

Add / Replace actions

Add/Replace targets

Add: store tonal context as progression metadata and/or add a Scale/Mode Pitch Set suggestion to library

Replace: set the progression’s active tonal context (and, if appropriate, update the linked Scale/Mode Pitch Set)

No automatic changes.

8) Minimal Data Model (Schema-ready, no code)
{
  "progression": {
    "mode": "byKey",
    "loop": true,
    "advanceRule": { "trigger": "perCorrect", "n": 1 },
    "steps": [
      { "degree": "I", "inheritChordSet": true },
      { "degree": "V", "inheritChordSet": true },
      { "degree": "vi", "inheritChordSet": true },
      { "degree": "IV", "inheritChordSet": true }
    ],
    "tonalContext": {
      "active": { "type": "mode", "name": "Ionian", "root": "C" },
      "library": []
    }
  }
}

Below is the canon update to the progression spec and the minimal schema additions needed to encode this cleanly (still no engine wiring).

Progressions System Design v1.1 Updates
1) Mode-Aware Degrees (Added)
What this means
A progression step may express its harmonic role relative to the active mode, not just major/minor defaults.

Step degree representation
Each step can specify:

degree: roman numeral (I, ii, V, etc.)

degreeContext: optional mode-aware label (i, ♭VII, II, etc.) depending on how you want to notate

Practical rule (keeps it deterministic)
The mode defines the scale-degree pitch pool

The step’s degree selects the scale degree

The step’s quality constraints (or inherited chord-set builder constraints) determine chord type

This gives flexibility without ambiguity.

2) Per-Step Override (Added)
Each step can optionally override progression-level defaults:

Override targets (allowed):

source override: byKey vs byRoot

tonal context override (advanced): root + mode/scale for that step only

chord constraint override: quality/extensions/alterations/inversions/voicing

bank restriction override: “bank-only” (force curated chords)

Guardrail (recommended default behavior)
Overrides are OFF by default and live behind the Step Details overlay so new users don’t hit complexity accidentally.

Updated Minimal Data Model (Schema-ready)
{
  "progression": {
    "mode": "byKey",
    "loop": true,
    "advanceRule": { "trigger": "perCorrect", "n": 1 },

    "tonalContext": {
      "active": { "type": "mode", "name": "Dorian", "root": "D" },
      "library": []
    },

    "steps": [
      {
        "degree": "i",
        "degreeContext": { "modeAware": true },

        "inheritChordSet": true,

        "overrides": {
          "enabled": false
        }
      },
      {
        "degree": "IV",
        "degreeContext": { "modeAware": true },

        "inheritChordSet": true,

        "overrides": {
          "enabled": true,
          "source": "byRoot",
          "rootPolicy": { "type": "fixed", "root": "F" },

          "constraints": {
            "qualities": ["min"],
            "extensions": ["7", "add9"],
            "alterations": ["b5"],
            "voicingTypes": ["open"],
            "inversions": ["root", "1st"]
          },

          "bankOnly": true
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}
Notes:

degreeContext.modeAware can be omitted when false.

Overrides are wrapped under overrides.enabled so the UI can keep a stable layout (disabled ≠ hidden).

This change does not require any layout redesign:

Mode-aware is a simple toggle/flag in Step Details

Overrides are a reserved “Overrides” block in Step Details (collapsed internally via disabled controls, not resizing)

muted/skipped

Schema notes (canon-aligned)
Supports progression-level mode and per-step override mode.

Step payload can carry both byKey and byRoot; the active one is determined by progression mode unless overridden.

Mode-aware degrees are represented by degreeContext.modeAware plus optional degreeLabel.

2) Examples Appendix
Each example shows how borrowed chords and secondary dominants are achieved using the existing override system.

A) Borrowed Chords (Modal Interchange)
Example: In C major, use iv (Fm) as a borrowed chord
Progression baseline:

mode: byKey

tonalContext: C Ionian (C major)

Steps:

I (C)

iv (Fm) (borrowed from C minor)

V (G)

I (C)

Implementation:

Step 2 sets overrides.enabled=true

overrides.tonalContext = { type:"mode", name:"Aeolian", root:"C" } (or “Natural Minor” if you prefer naming)

overrides.constraints.qualities=["min"]

byKey.degree="iv" with degreeContext.modeAware=true

Result: Still “By Key,” but step 2 temporarily borrows from parallel minor.

B) Secondary Dominants
Example: In C major, use V/V (D7) resolving to V (G)
Steps:

I (C)

V/V (D7) (secondary dominant)

V (G)

I (C)

Implementation options (both valid):

Option 1 — By Root override (simple and direct)

Step 2: overrides.source="byRoot"

overrides.constraints.qualities=["maj"] + overrides.constraints.seventh=["dom7"]

byRoot.rootPolicy={type:"fixed", root:"D"}

Option 2 — By Key with a targeted degree label (theory-forward)

Keep progression byKey

Step 2 uses degreeLabel="V/V" with degreeContext.modeAware=true

overrides.constraints.seventh=["dom7"]

(Engine later resolves V/V to a dominant built on the 2nd degree’s dominant target)

For v1 design, Option 1 is the most deterministic; Option 2 is supported as notation and future resolution.

C) Borrowed + Secondary Combined (Common in real music)
Example: In C major: I → ♭VII → IV → V/V → V → I
♭VII (B♭ major) is borrowed (Mixolydian flavor)

V/V is secondary dominant

Implementation:

Step 2 override tonalContext to C Mixolydian (or allow non-diatonic root via byRoot)

Step 4 override to byRoot D7

D) “Advanced player doesn’t get frustrated” proof
A player can:

stay in a beginner-safe byKey diatonic locked progression,

then selectively open Overrides on only the steps they want for color,

without forcing the whole game into “theory chaos.”

That matches your design intent exactly.

where progressions appear in Basic vs Advanced (likely Advanced only),

how the selected progression affects prompt generation,

and how Tonal Assist “Add/Replace” writes into progression metadata vs active context.
